  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● I read the whole repo: the server code, Prisma schema and migration, seed, the
  one test file, the Dockerfile, docker-compose and README. I haven't changed
  any files.

  What's there now

  The server is small: 3 routers, 1 service, 1 middleware, 1 helpers file and 1
  test file that covers only the pure helpers. The main structural problems:

  - Business logic lives in route handlers. Only events have a service, and
    getEventWithSeats(req) takes an Express Request.
  - index.ts builds the app and calls listen(). Tests can't import the app
    without starting a server.
  - Config is scattered. The JWT secret is hardcoded in routes/auth.ts twice and
    again as a fallback in middleware/auth.ts. PORT is read inline.
  - Error responses are inconsistent. The shapes are {error}, {message}, {msg},
    a plain string, and 200 {success:false} for a missing event. There is no
    central error handler, and some handlers have no try/catch.
  - There is no input validation. The routes read req.body directly.
  - The Docker setup is dev-only. The Dockerfile runs tsx watch and seeds on
    every container start.

  I'm listing three real bugs only because the restructure will touch those
  files. I won't fix them as part of it unless you say so:

  - Cancelling a booking doesn't check ownership. Any logged-in user can cancel
    anyone's booking.
  - Booking has a check-then-insert race. Two users can book the same seat at
    the same time.
  - listEvents counts cancelled seats as taken. It includes all bookings, not
    just confirmed ones.

  Proposed structure

  I'd keep it as a layered feature-based layout under server/src, with no DI
  container, repository layer or classes.

  server/
  ├── prisma/
  │   ├── schema.prisma            (unchanged)
  │   ├── migrations/              (unchanged)
  │   └── seed.ts                  (unchanged, import path adjusted)
  ├── src/
  │   ├── server.ts                # entry: load config, app.listen, graceful
  shutdown
  │   ├── app.ts                   # createApp(): middleware, routes, 404, error
  handler
  │   ├── config/
  │   │   └── env.ts               # one typed env object (PORT, DATABASE_URL,
  JWT_SECRET, ...)
  │   ├── lib/
  │   │   ├── prisma.ts            # PrismaClient singleton (was db.ts)
  │   │   ├── errors.ts            # AppError (+ NotFound/Forbidden/Conflict
  helpers)
  │   │   └── jwt.ts               # signToken / verifyToken
  │   ├── middleware/
  │   │   ├── auth.ts              # requireAuth, requireRole('ORGANIZER')
  │   │   ├── validate.ts          # validate(zodSchema) for body/params
  │   │   └── errorHandler.ts      # one place that shapes every error response
  │   ├── modules/
  │   │   ├── auth/
  │   │   │   ├── auth.routes.ts       # wiring only
  │   │   │   ├── auth.controller.ts   # req/res <-> service
  │   │   │   ├── auth.service.ts      # register, login
  │   │   │   └── auth.schemas.ts      # zod schemas
  │   │   ├── events/
  │   │   │   ├── events.routes.ts
  │   │   │   ├── events.controller.ts
  │   │   │   ├── events.service.ts    # list, getById, create (+ seat grid)
  │   │   │   └── events.schemas.ts
  │   │   └── bookings/
  │   │       ├── bookings.routes.ts
  │   │       ├── bookings.controller.ts
  │   │       ├── bookings.service.ts  # create, listMine, cancel
  │   │       └── bookings.schemas.ts
  │   ├── utils/
  │   │   └── helpers.ts           # formatPrice, seatLabel, isSeatBookable,
  slugify
  │   └── types/
  │       └── express.d.ts         # augment Request with `user` (drops
  AuthRequest)
  ├── test/
  │   ├── unit/helpers.test.ts     # the existing test, moved
  │   └── integration/             # supertest against createApp(); auth,
  events, bookings
  ├── Dockerfile                   # multi-stage: dev target + production target
  ├── .env.example                 # adds JWT_SECRET
  └── package.json                 # adds zod, supertest; "build"/"start" via
  tsc

  At the repo root, docker-compose.yml stays as the dev setup. I'd add an
  optional docker-compose.prod.yml only if you want a production run.

  Decisions and trade-offs

  - Modules are split by feature, with routes → controller → service inside
    each.
    - Routes only wire up paths.
    - Controllers translate HTTP to service calls.
    - Services hold the logic and the Prisma calls.
    - Only src/ is restructured. Controllers and services mean more files, but
      that is what makes the logic testable without Express.
  - I'd skip a repository layer. Prisma already is the data layer, and wrapping
    it would be overengineering at this size.
  - app.ts and server.ts are split so supertest can import the app without
    opening a port.
  - config/env.ts validates env at startup. In production it fails fast if
    JWT_SECRET is missing. The dev fallback stays in dev only, so local and
    Docker runs keep working.
  - Zod is the only new runtime dependency. It gives validation and typed
    inputs.
  - One error handler with one response shape. I'd go with { error: string },
    since that's the most common shape today. That shape changes some responses,
    so I need to check what the client reads before standardising.
  - The Dockerfile gets two stages.
    - The dev target keeps today's behaviour, including migrate, seed and tsx
      watch.
    - The production target runs tsc, then node dist/server.js, with prisma
      migrate deploy but no seed.
    - Compose uses the dev target, so docker compose up --build works as before.
  - The API paths, the database schema and the seed data stay the same. The
    README's API table stays valid.

  Questions for you

  1. Behavior fixes: do you want the three bugs fixed during the refactor, or in
     a separate follow-up? The ownership check and the race are small once
     they're in services. The race fix is a transaction plus a partial unique
     index, which means a new migration.
  2. Error shape: are you happy standardising on { error }? If so, I'll check
     client/src for any message or msg reads before changing anything.
  3. Layout: do you want the layered structure above, or something flatter, with
     routes/ and services/ but no controllers? The flatter option is fewer
     files but keeps HTTP handling mixed into the routes.

  Once you answer, I'll implement in small steps and run typecheck, tests and
  the Docker build after each one.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Env validation now runs before the server starts listening. The Docker server
  picked up the change through its volume mount and still serves /health.
  Typecheck and the 3 tests pass.

  What it does: validateEnv() in config/env.ts is called from server.ts before
  app.listen. It collects every problem, prints them all, and exits with code 1.
  - DATABASE_URL: required, and must start with postgres:// or postgresql://.
  - PORT: must be an integer from 1 to 65535 if set. The default of 4000 is
    unchanged.
  - JWT_SECRET: required only when NODE_ENV=production. Without that, the dev
    default still applies, so Docker and local runs behave as before.

  I wrote this by hand with no new dependency. Zod would be overkill for three
  checks.

  One addition you didn't ask for: env.ts now calls Node's built-in
  process.loadEnvFile() to read server/.env if it exists. Prisma only reads .env
  when it connects, and tsx doesn't read it at all. So without this, the
  README's non-Docker flow (cp .env.example .env && npm run dev) would fail
  validation on a missing DATABASE_URL. Docker is unaffected, and variables that
  are already set are never overridden.

  Runs against the real server code:

  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┐
  │                  Case                   │            Result             │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ No DATABASE_URL                         │ exit 1, "DATABASE_URL is      │
  │                                         │ required"                     │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ Bad URL, PORT=abc, production without a │ exit 1, all three errors      │
  │  secret                                 │ listed                        │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ Valid env vars                          │ starts normally               │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ .env file only, no env vars             │ starts normally               │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ Docker compose                          │ starts normally               │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────┘

● I found that /bookings/mine badges every row (so holds/expired holds need a
  separate active-holds endpoint) and that there are no duplicate confirmed
  seats, so a unique index is safe to add. Now I'll write the schema migrations,
  splitting the new enum values and the index that depends on them into
  separate migrations since Postgres can't use a new enum value in the same
  transaction that creates it. · summary

● Seat holding is in. A held seat is unavailable to everyone else for 10
  minutes, expired holds free the seat on their own, and concurrent requests
  can't double-book a seat. I tested it against the running Docker stack with 34
  live checks, all passing, plus typecheck and the 3 existing unit tests. Those
  unit tests don't cover holds; the live script does, but it isn't saved in the
  repo.

  Schema and migrations:
  - BookingStatus gains HELD and EXPIRED, and Booking gains expiresAt.
  - Two migrations, because Postgres can't use a new enum value in the same
    transaction that adds it. The second adds a partial unique index so a seat
    can have only one CONFIRMED or HELD booking at a time.
  - Concurrency rests on that index. Prisma can't express it in schema.prisma,
    so it exists only in the migration SQL. I checked with prisma migrate dev
    --create-only and Prisma reports no drift.
  - No existing seat had duplicate confirmed bookings, so the index applied
    cleanly.

  API: the new endpoints sit alongside the existing ones.

  ┌────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │        Endpoint        │                    Behavior                    │
  ├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ POST /bookings/hold    │ Holds a seat for 10 minutes. Holding a seat    │
  │                        │ you already hold returns the same hold.        │
  ├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ POST                   │ Turns your unexpired hold into a booking.      │
  │ /bookings/:id/confirm  │ Another user's hold gives 404, an expired hold │
  │                        │  410, and an already-confirmed booking 409.    │
  ├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ GET /bookings/holds    │ Lists your active holds.                       │
  ├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ POST /bookings         │ Still books directly. It fails with 409 if     │
  │ (existing)             │ someone else holds the seat, and confirms your │
  │                        │  own hold if you have one.                     │
  └────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Expiry: it works in three ways, so no single piece has to run for it to hold.
  - Event listings, event detail and the booking checks treat an expired hold as
    free the moment it lapses.
  - A booking attempt marks any stale hold on that seat EXPIRED first.
  - A sweeper in server.ts marks expired holds every 30 seconds.

  Live results:
  - Concurrency: 12 parallel requests on one seat gave exactly one 201, whether
    all holds, all direct bookings, or a mix. The database also rejects a second
    active row inserted directly.
  - Expiry: I backdated expiresAt in SQL instead of waiting 10 minutes. A lapsed
    hold freed the seat immediately, and the sweeper marked one EXPIRED within
    35 seconds.
  - Existing flow: direct book, taken seat (409), cancel and re-book all work.
  - Cleanup: I deleted the test event and users.

  Things to know:
  - Seat counts change: the event list used to count cancelled seats as taken,
    so it showed fewer seats left than the detail page did. Filtering to active
    bookings fixes that as a side effect, and the list now matches the detail
    page.
  - Cancel is unchanged: it only affects confirmed bookings, and anyone logged
    in can still cancel any booking. I left it alone, as before. Holds can't be
    cancelled early, only confirmed or left to expire.
  - No UI yet: the client is untouched. It still books directly through POST
    /bookings, and /bookings/mine omits holds so the existing page renders as
    before. A hold and confirm flow in the UI is a separate step.
  - Not committed: nothing is committed.

  current functionalities work fine after the modification.

  Event page (EventDetailPage.tsx (client/src/pages/EventDetailPage.tsx))

    holds the selected seat. If you're not logged in it sends you to /login,
    same as booking.

    seat, a live countdown and a Confirm button. Holds are reloaded on page
    load, so they survive a refresh.

    re-selected. Seats held by others show as unavailable, because the server
    already reports them that way.

    expired, and the seat map refreshes so the seat shows as free. If your clock
    and the server's differ, the server decides. A late confirm gets the
    server's "Hold has expired" error as a toast.

    this file with the VITE_API_URL setting the other pages use. The default is
    the same.

    green toast.

  turns red under a minute and calls back once when it expires.

> In the backend, make sure one person can hold max two sits at a time. One

● The limit needs two things on the server:
  - Per-user lock: a lock on the user's row while a hold is created, so two
    simultaneous requests can't both pass the count check.
  - Count check: a check of the user's active holds inside the same transaction.

  That makes a schema change unnecessary, because a stored counter would only
  duplicate state and could drift. Let me make the service change.

● One person can now hold at most 2 seats at a time, and concurrent requests
  can't get past it. No schema change was needed. Typecheck is clean, and on the
  rebuilt server the new HTTP check and the earlier 34-check regression script
  both pass.

  How it works (in bookings.service.ts and bookings.rules.ts):
  - Per-user lock: a hold request takes a row lock on the user (SELECT … FOR
    UPDATE) at the start of its transaction, so one user's simultaneous holds
    run one at a time. Different users still run in parallel.
  - Count check: inside that lock, the request counts the user's unexpired
    holds. If they already have MAX_ACTIVE_HOLDS (2), it returns
    HOLD_LIMIT_REACHED.
  - Response: the endpoint answers 409 with You can hold at most 2 seats at a
    time. The client already shows data.error as a toast, so the message appears
    with no client change.
  - Why no schema change: a stored counter would only duplicate state that can
    drift. The count comes straight from the booking rows.

  What counts toward the limit:
  - Counted: only active, unexpired holds, across all events.
  - Not counted: expired holds, confirmed holds and direct bookings.
  - Always allowed: at the limit you can still re-hold a seat you already hold,
    book directly, or confirm a hold. Confirming a hold frees a slot.

  Tests: holdLimit.test.ts (server/test/holdLimit.test.ts) has 10 tests that run
  the real service against Postgres, since mocks couldn't prove a concurrency
  guarantee. They create their own users and events and delete them afterwards.
  They cover:
  - the limit and the rejection of the next hold
  - 10 parallel holds by one user ending with exactly 2
  - repeated bursts
  - the limit applying per user
  - the limit counting across events
  - expired holds not counting
  - confirmed holds and direct bookings not counting
  - re-holding your own seat at the limit
  - direct booking at the limit
  - confirming a hold freeing a slot

  Verification:
  - Mutation check: with the lock temporarily removed, the two concurrency tests
    failed on all 3 runs. With it restored, all 13 tests (3 old, 10 new) passed
    on 3 consecutive runs.
  - HTTP check: 6 parallel hold requests from one user gave 2 created and 4
    rejected with 409.
  - Skipping: without a DATABASE_URL, the 10 DB tests skip and the 3 helper
    tests pass. To run them: cd server && npm test with a .env, or docker
    compose exec server npm test.

  Dev note: the server container was still running the old code after my edit,
  because tsx watch often misses file changes on Windows bind mounts. My first
  HTTP check let 6 holds through for that reason. After docker compose restart
  server the limit was enforced. If code changes don't seem to take effect in
  Docker, restart the server container.

  Nothing is committed.

● All 22 tests pass, and removing the new check makes 4 of them fail. Now the

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Once a user's hold on a seat expires, that seat is now unavailable to them,
  and they get a 403 if they try to hold or book it. Typecheck is clean, and all
  22 tests pass (3 old helper tests, 10 hold-limit tests, 9 new ones). The live
  server passes my earlier 34-check script plus a direct reproduction.

  First I checked for a bug. I reproduced the scenario: hold a seat, let the
  hold lapse, then book or re-hold it as the same user. It worked: the seat
  freed up and the same user could take it again. So I read your message as a
  rule you want. If you meant "the seat wrongly becomes unavailable to me", tell
  me and I'll revert this.

  What changed (bookings.service.ts and bookings.controller.ts):
  - The rule: when a hold lapses it is marked EXPIRED. A hold or direct booking
    on a seat now checks for an EXPIRED row of the same user on that seat. If
    one exists, it returns 403 with "Your hold on this seat expired, so it is no
    longer available to you".
  - Lapsed but not yet swept: a hold that has lapsed but hasn't been swept yet
    is marked EXPIRED first, so it blocks from the instant it lapses.
  - Everyone else: other users can hold or book the seat normally.
  - Other seats: the block is per seat and per user. That user can still hold or
    book other seats.
  - Not blocked: confirmed bookings that were later cancelled don't block, and
    neither do seats the user never held.
  - Confirm: confirming a lapsed hold still returns 410, as before.
  - Schema: no change.

  Tests are in holdExpiry.test.ts (server/test/holdExpiry.test.ts). They cover:
  - hold and direct booking blocked after a lapse
  - still blocked after the sweeper runs
  - the confirm 410
  - other users unaffected
  - other seats unaffected
  - cancelled bookings and never-held seats not blocked
  - the active-hold case unchanged

  With the new check disabled, 4 of them fail, so they do catch it. The user and
  event setup moved into test/support/fixtures.ts, and the hold-limit test now
  uses it.

  Things to know:
  - Permanent block: the block is for good, not for a cooldown. A user who lets
    a hold lapse can never get that seat. If you want a time limit instead,
    that's a small change.
  - Client unchanged: the seat map is public and has no user in it, so a blocked
    user still sees the seat as free. They find out from the 403 message when
    they click Hold or Book Now. Showing it as unavailable for them would need
    the seat map to know who is asking.
  - Restart in dev: I restarted the server container again after the edit,
    because tsx watch misses changes on Windows bind mounts.

  Nothing is committed.

● No, another user cannot book a seat that someone else is holding. I ran the
  server tests against the real Postgres database, and all 28 pass. The 6 new
  tests in holdExclusivity.test.ts (server/test/holdExclusivity.test.ts) cover
  this case, and the earlier tests still pass.

  ┌───────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┐
  │             Scenario              │               Result                │
  ├───────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Another user tries to book a held │ Rejected with SEAT_HELD. The        │
  │  seat                             │ database still has only the         │
  │                                   │ holder's HELD row.                  │
  ├───────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Another user tries to hold a held │ Rejected with SEAT_HELD.            │
  │  seat                             │                                     │
  ├───────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ The holder confirms after others  │ Works. The seat ends up CONFIRMED   │
  │ were rejected                     │ for the holder.                     │
  ├───────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 10 users try to book a held seat  │ All 10 fail, and the holder's hold  │
  │ at the same moment                │ is untouched.                       │
  ├───────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ One user holds while another      │ Exactly one wins, and only one      │
  │ books the same free seat at the   │ active row exists.                  │
  │ same moment                       │                                     │
  ├───────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Another user tries after the hold │ They can book it.                   │
  │  has expired                      │                                     │
  └───────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────┘

  Over HTTP the same rejection comes back as 409 {"error":"Seat is currently on
  hold"}. I saw that on the live server in the earlier manual checks. This run
  exercises the service layer, so it doesn't re-test the HTTP layer.

  The tests create their own users and events and delete them afterwards, and no
  test rows were left in the database. To rerun, use cd server && npm test with
  a .env pointing at the database, or docker compose exec server npm test.
  Without a DATABASE_URL, the database tests skip.

  I haven't committed anything.

● Seats that are blocked for you because your own hold expired now show as
  disabled on the event page. I haven't opened the page in a browser, so the
  look is unchecked. The client typechecks and builds, all 29 server tests pass,
  and the earlier 34-check script still passes.

  The seat map is public and doesn't know who is viewing, so this needed one
  small server addition.

  Server
  - New endpoint: GET /bookings/blocked-seats?eventId= needs login. It returns
    the seats you can no longer take because your hold on them lapsed, including
    holds the sweeper hasn't marked yet.
  - Live check: before the hold expired it returned [], and after it returned
    the seat for the holder. For another user it returned [], a bad eventId gave
    400, and no token gave 401.
  - New test: a test in holdExpiry.test.ts covers swept and unswept holds, the
    per-event filter, and another user seeing nothing.
  - README: I added the endpoint to the README's API table.

  Event page (EventDetailPage.tsx (client/src/pages/EventDetailPage.tsx))
  - Look: a blocked seat has a dashed border, a faint fill, struck-through text
    and a not-allowed cursor.
  - Behavior: it ignores clicks, has no hover effect and can't be selected for
    Book Now or Hold. Hovering shows "Your hold on this seat expired, so it is
    no longer available to you".
  - Timing: the list loads with the page. When a countdown hits zero, that seat
    is disabled immediately, without waiting for a refetch.
  - Legend: a small legend line appears under the grid when any seat is blocked.

  Unchanged
  - Taken and other users' held seats look as before.
  - Your own active holds are still amber.

  The blocked style is different from a taken seat on purpose. Taken seats stay
  faded but clickable, as they were before.

  I restarted the server and client containers so they picked up the changes.
  Nothing is committed.

      257              ))}
      258            </div>

● Booked and held seats are now unselectable on the event page. The change is UI
  only, in EventDetailPage.tsx (client/src/pages/EventDetailPage.tsx). The
  client typechecks and builds, but I haven't opened the page in a browser, so
  the look and click behavior are unchecked.

  What changed
  - Rule: a seat can only be selected if the server reports it as available and
    it isn't one of your expired-hold seats. Clicking any other seat does
    nothing.
  - Look: unselectable seats show a not-allowed cursor, with no hover grow or
    color change. Hovering a booked or held seat shows "This seat is not
    available".
  - Unchanged: the faded style for booked seats, the amber for your own holds,
    and the dashed style for your expired-hold seats.
  - Selection cleanup: a seat you've selected is now deselected if someone else
    takes it before you act. Previously it would stay highlighted as selected.

  Before this change, clicking a booked seat selected it, and you only learned
  it was taken from the error after clicking Book Now. That can no longer happen
  from the UI. The server still rejects any request for a taken seat.

  Nothing is committed.

  Searched for 1 pattern (ctrl+o to expand)

● I changed no files. The only things I created were temporary logs in /tmp and
  throwaway server instances on port 4996, which I killed afterwards. The Docker
  stack was only probed with read-only requests. Items marked ✅ I reproduced;
  the rest come from reading the code.

  Critical

  C1 ✅ Anyone can forge a login token
  - Where: auth.service.ts:6 (server/src/modules/auth/auth.service.ts#L6),
    env.ts:11 (server/src/config/env.ts#L11), env.ts:29
    (server/src/config/env.ts#L29)
  - Problem: tokens are signed with the literal 'seatbook-secret-123', which is
    in the repo. The NODE_ENV=production check never fires because neither the
    Dockerfile nor compose sets it. I signed a token with that secret for user 1
    (organizer) and the live API accepted it (200).
  - Impact: anyone can impersonate any user, including organizers.
  - Trap: signing uses the literal but verifying uses JWT_SECRET. Setting
    JWT_SECRET breaks every login (all later requests get 401) instead of fixing
    the problem.
  - Fix:
    - Use one secret from the environment for both signing and verifying.
    - Remove the default and require it at startup.
    - Pin the algorithm to HS256.
    - Rotate the secret and set NODE_ENV=production in the production image.

  C2 ✅ One request can kill the API process
  - Where:
    - events.controller.ts:11
      (server/src/modules/events/events.controller.ts#L11): GET /events/abc
    - auth.controller.ts:21 (server/src/modules/auth/auth.controller.ts#L21):
      POST /auth/login with a missing or non-string field
    - bookings.controller.ts:110
      (server/src/modules/bookings/bookings.controller.ts#L110): POST
      /bookings/abc/cancel
    - events.controller.ts:6
      (server/src/modules/events/events.controller.ts#L6): GET /events (a DB
      error would do it)
  - Problem: Express 4 doesn't catch errors thrown in async handlers, and there
    is no global error handler. Node exits on the resulting unhandled rejection.
    I confirmed the instance returned 200 on /health, then died after GET
    /events/abc. The other two vectors also killed it.
  - Impact: an unauthenticated denial of service, and there is no restart
    policy.
  - Fix:
    - Add an async wrapper, or upgrade to Express 5.
    - Add one error-handling middleware.
    - Validate params and bodies before use.
    - Add unhandledRejection logging and graceful shutdown.
    - Run under a restart policy.

  High

  H1 Anyone can cancel anyone's booking
  - Where: bookings.service.ts:199
    (server/src/modules/bookings/bookings.service.ts#L199) and
    bookings.controller.ts:110
    (server/src/modules/bookings/bookings.controller.ts#L110)
  - Problem: the update filters on booking id only, and the response is
    {success:true} even when nothing changed. IDs are sequential integers.
  - Impact: any logged-in user can cancel every confirmed booking by counting
    upward.
  - Fix: add userId: req.user.id to the update, and return 404 when no row
    matched.

  H2 Anyone can register as an organizer
  - Where: auth.service.ts:20 (server/src/modules/auth/auth.service.ts#L20)
  - Problem: the role field in the register body is honored.
  - Impact: organizers can create unlimited events, each with up to 780 seats,
    so anyone can bloat the database.
  - Fix: ignore role on register, and create organizers through the seed, an
    admin action or an approval step.

  H3 ✅ Internal errors are sent to clients
  - Where: auth.controller.ts:16
    (server/src/modules/auth/auth.controller.ts#L16), bookings.controller.ts
    (server/src/modules/bookings/bookings.controller.ts) lines 40, 50, 76, 93
    and 106, and app.ts (server/src/app.ts) (no error middleware)
  - Problem:
    - A duplicate register returns the Prisma message with the source file path
      and a code excerpt.
    - Malformed JSON returns Express's default HTML page with a stack trace,
      because NODE_ENV is unset.
    - X-Powered-By: Express is exposed.
    - The duplicate-register message also confirms which emails exist.
  - Fix:
    - Use one error handler that maps known errors (for example Prisma P2002
      to 409) to fixed messages.
    - Never send err.message.
    - Disable x-powered-by, add helmet, and set NODE_ENV=production.

  H4 ✅ Dependency advisory in the body parser
  - Where: server/package.json, via express ^4.22.3
  - Problem: npm audit reports 1 high-severity issue, GHSA-27r3-jxmh-p828, in
    body-parser below 1.20.9. It allows denial of service through trailing bytes
    after a compressed request body.
  - Fix: run npm audit fix or bump express. I didn't audit the client; its
    node_modules isn't installed on the host.

  H5 Default database credentials and seeded accounts
  - Where: docker-compose.yml:4 (docker-compose.yml#L4) and docker-compose.yml:8
    (docker-compose.yml#L8), and server/Dockerfile:9 (server/Dockerfile#L9)
  - Problem:
    - The database password is seatbook, and port 5433 is published on all host
      interfaces.
    - The only image runs the seed and tsx watch as root on every start.
    - On an empty database, that creates organizer@seatbook.test / password123.
  - Fix:
    - Keep a dev compose file separate from a production one.
    - Bind the database port to 127.0.0.1, or don't publish it.
    - Take credentials from secrets.
    - Seed only in dev.
    - Add a production image stage that runs as a non-root user and starts with
      node dist/….

  H6 GET /events does unbounded work with no login
  - Where: events.service.ts:6 (server/src/modules/events/events.service.ts#L6)
  - Problem: every call loads all events, all seats and all active bookings into
    memory.
  - Impact: cost grows with total seats and can be abused for denial of service.
  - Fix: compute seats left in SQL with counts or groupBy, add pagination, and
    cache briefly.

  Medium: validation

  M1 Event creation accepts bad values
  - Where: events.validation.ts:6
    (server/src/modules/events/events.validation.ts#L6) and events.service.ts:77
    (server/src/modules/events/events.service.ts#L77)
  - Problem: the checks are truthiness only.
    - Negative rows or cols pass and produce an event with zero seats.
    - Non-integers and strings give a Prisma error and a 500.
    - Invalid or past startsAt is accepted.
    - A negative price displays as "$-5.00".
    - There are no length limits on title, venue or description.
  - Fix: use schema validation (zod) with integer ranges, a future date,
    priceCents of at least 0 and string limits. Add DB CHECK constraints too.

  M2 Auth input checks are too thin
  - Where: auth.validation.ts:3 (server/src/modules/auth/auth.validation.ts#L3),
    auth.controller.ts:22 (server/src/modules/auth/auth.controller.ts#L22),
    schema.prisma (server/prisma/schema.prisma) (User.email)
  - Problem:
    - There is no email format check, no password minimum and no type check.
    - Login has no validation at all, which feeds C2.
    - Emails are case-sensitive, so A@x.com and a@x.com are separate accounts.
    - bcrypt silently truncates passwords at 72 bytes.
  - Fix: trim and lowercase emails, enforce a minimum password length, validate
    types, and use a case-insensitive unique index.

  M3 seatId and path ids are parsed loosely
  - Where: bookings.controller.ts:36
    (server/src/modules/bookings/bookings.controller.ts#L36) and
    bookings.controller.ts:46
    (server/src/modules/bookings/bookings.controller.ts#L46)
  - Problem: Number(req.body.seatId) turns undefined into NaN, which gives a 500
    with a leaked message. 1.5 also gives a 500. true becomes seat 1 and [5]
    becomes 5. parseInt("12abc") gives 12.
  - Fix: parse positive integers strictly and return 400 on failure.

  M4 No rate limiting
  - Where: app.ts:9 (server/src/app.ts#L9)
  - Problem: login, register, booking and hold requests are unthrottled.
  - Impact: passwords can be brute-forced. Free sign-ups let one person create
    many accounts and get around the 2-hold limit.
  - Fix: add per-IP and per-account limits, and optionally email verification.

  Medium: business logic and data integrity

  M5 Booking limits have gaps
  - Where: bookings.service.ts:84
    (server/src/modules/bookings/bookings.service.ts#L84),
    bookings.service.ts:112
    (server/src/modules/bookings/bookings.service.ts#L112),
    bookings.service.ts:36 (server/src/modules/bookings/bookings.service.ts#L36)
  - Problem:
    - The 2-seat cap applies only to holds, so one user can directly book every
      seat in an event.
    - Holds can also be cycled across different seats forever.
    - confirmHold never checks that the event hasn't started.
    - A hold can outlive the event start.
  - Fix: add a per-event booking cap (the exact limit is your decision). Check
    the event start in confirmHold, and cap expiresAt at the event start.

  M6 Auth middleware trusts the token completely
  - Where: auth.ts:15 (server/src/middleware/auth.ts#L15)
  - Problem: it never checks the payload shape or that the user still exists,
    and takes the role from the token. Deleted or demoted users keep access for
    7 days, and there is no revocation. .replace('Bearer ', '') also accepts a
    bare token.
  - Fix: validate claims, parse the Bearer scheme strictly, and either shorten
    token lifetime with refresh or look the user up per request.

  M7 The double-booking guard exists only in a hand-written migration
  - Where: server/prisma/migrations/20261028000100_seat_single_active_booking,
    and schema.prisma (server/prisma/schema.prisma) (nothing mentions it)
  - Problem: Prisma can't model the partial unique index. prisma db push or a
    reset from the schema alone would silently drop it, and the race protection
    would be gone.
  - Fix: comment it in schema.prisma and the README. The integration tests would
    catch its loss, but only if they run in CI.

  M8 Gaps in the database design
  - Where: schema.prisma (server/prisma/schema.prisma) (Booking, Event)
  - Problem:
    - There are no indexes on Booking.userId or Booking.seatId, and Postgres
      doesn't index foreign keys. The "mine", "holds" and hold-limit queries
      scan the table.
    - There are no CHECK constraints on rows, cols or priceCents.
    - EXPIRED rows grow forever, and the "seat blocked after your hold expires"
      rule depends on them, so they can't simply be purged.
  - Fix: add the indexes and constraints, and plan a retention approach for
    expired rows, such as a separate block table.

  M9 Errors are hidden or mislabeled
  - Where: bookings.controller.ts:83
    (server/src/modules/bookings/bookings.controller.ts#L83) and
    events.controller.ts:15 (server/src/modules/events/events.controller.ts#L15)
  - Problem:
    - listMine returns [] with 200 on any error, so a database outage looks like
      "no bookings" and a user may rebook.
    - A missing event returns 200 with {success:false}.
    - Error bodies use four shapes: error, message, msg and plain text.
  - Fix: return real status codes (500 and 404), and use one error envelope.

  Medium: client

  M10 The client crashes or hangs on failed requests
  - Where:
    - EventDetailPage.tsx:26 (client/src/pages/EventDetailPage.tsx#L26) and
      EventDetailPage.tsx:31 (client/src/pages/EventDetailPage.tsx#L31): an
      unknown event returns 200 {success:false}, then data.seats.forEach throws
      and the spinner never ends.
    - MyBookingsPage.tsx:33 (client/src/pages/MyBookingsPage.tsx#L33) and
      MyBookingsPage.tsx:39 (client/src/pages/MyBookingsPage.tsx#L39): an
      expired or invalid token returns a 401 object, data.forEach fails and
      loading never ends.
    - LoginPage.tsx:20 (client/src/pages/LoginPage.tsx#L20): there is no
      try/catch around the request.
    - EventDetailPage.tsx:109 (client/src/pages/EventDetailPage.tsx#L109):
      data.error shows "undefined" for msg-shaped errors such as "event already
      started".
    - CreateEventPage.tsx:40 (client/src/pages/CreateEventPage.tsx#L40): it
      shows raw JSON as the error.
    - CreateEventPage.tsx:35 (client/src/pages/CreateEventPage.tsx#L35): a price
      typo becomes NaN, then null, then silently $0.
    - Navbar.tsx:6 (client/src/components/Navbar.tsx#L6): unguarded JSON.parse
      can white-screen the whole app.
  - Fix: check res.ok everywhere, catch network errors, redirect to /login on
    401, and read a single error shape.

  M11 The login token is stored in localStorage
  - Where: LoginPage.tsx:27 (client/src/pages/LoginPage.tsx#L27)
  - Problem: any XSS bug would steal a 7-day token. I found no
    dangerouslySetInnerHTML, eval or innerHTML in the client, so there is no
    known XSS today.
  - Fix: use httpOnly secure cookies with CSRF protection, or shorten the token
    lifetime.

  Low and hardening

  - ✅ CORS: it allows any origin (app.ts:9 (server/src/app.ts#L9)). Restrict it
    to the app's origin in production.
  - Login timing: auth.service.ts:29
    (server/src/modules/auth/auth.service.ts#L29) only runs bcrypt when the user
    exists, so response time reveals which emails are registered.
  - Shutdown: server.ts (server/src/server.ts) has no graceful shutdown, no
    Prisma disconnect and no unhandledRejection handler.
  - Lock contention: under heavy per-user lock contention, Prisma's default
    5-second transaction timeout could return 500s.
  - Config: server/.env.example is missing JWT_SECRET, and LoginPage.tsx:15
    (client/src/pages/LoginPage.tsx#L15) still hardcodes the API URL.
  - Docker: neither Dockerfile has a HEALTHCHECK. The client image runs the Vite
    dev server.
  - Test gaps: the tests cover the hold, limit and expiry logic well, but
    nothing tests HTTP-level auth. Cancel ownership and role checks are
    untested.

  Suggested order

  1. C1 and C2: one secret in one place, plus an async wrapper and error
     middleware. This also fixes much of H3 and M9.
  2. H1 and H2: cancel ownership and the register role.
  3. M1 to M3: validation, ideally in one pass with zod.
  4. H4 to H6: dependency bump, production config and the events query.
  5. M5 to M11: the business rules, database constraints and client error
     handling.

  I can start on any of these when you say.

  minimal.

● The JWT secret is no longer hardcoded: signing and verifying both use
  JWT_SECRET, and the server refuses to start without it. Typecheck passes and
  all 44 server tests pass (18 of them need no database).

  What changed
  - One signing and verifying path: the new lib/jwt.ts (server/src/lib/jwt.ts)
    is the only code that touches jsonwebtoken. auth.service.ts
    (server/src/modules/auth/auth.service.ts) calls signToken and
    middleware/auth.ts (server/src/middleware/auth.ts) calls verifyToken. The
    literal 'seatbook-secret-123' now appears nowhere in src/.
  - Algorithm pinned: tokens are signed and verified as HS256 only, which was a
    one-line addition. Tokens using alg: none or a different HMAC algorithm are
    rejected.
  - No default secret: in env.ts (server/src/config/env.ts), a missing, empty or
    blank JWT_SECRET stops startup with JWT_SECRET is required. It is required
    in every environment, not just production, and a server with no secret can
    no longer start.
  - Testable validation: the checks moved into getEnvErrors(env), which
    validateEnv calls. That lets tests check them without the process exiting.
  - Config files:
    - .env.example (server/.env.example) has a JWT_SECRET placeholder.
    - The README says the server won't start without one.
    - docker-compose.yml passes JWT_SECRET. Otherwise docker compose up would
      fail now.

  Tests:
  - env.test.ts (server/test/env.test.ts) has 6 tests: a complete environment
    passes; missing, empty and blank secrets fail, including outside production;
    all problems are reported at once; and the DATABASE_URL and PORT checks
    still work.
  - jwt.test.ts (server/test/jwt.test.ts) has 9 tests:
    - Tokens are signed with JWT_SECRET and a sign-then-verify round trip works.
    - Tokens signed with the old hardcoded secret, another secret, alg: none or
      HS512 are rejected.
    - Expiry is 7 days.
    - requireAuth accepts a valid token and returns 401 for the old-secret
      token, no token and garbage.
  - I tried two regressions, putting the hardcoded secret back into signing and
    putting the default and optional check back into env.ts. Each made 4 tests
    fail, and both are restored.

  Live check:
  - A fresh login token works.
  - A token forged with the old secret now gets 401. That forged token was
    accepted before.
  - Register still works end to end. I removed the test user afterwards.
  - Starting without a secret, or with a blank one, exits with code 1.

  Things to know
  - Everyone is logged out once. Tokens issued before this change were signed
    with the old secret, so users get 401 and need to log in again.
  - Compose still has a dev secret. It falls back to
    dev-only-jwt-secret-change-me unless you set JWT_SECRET in your shell or a
    root .env. That keeps docker compose up --build working locally. It is
    public in the repo, so don't use it anywhere shared.
  - Not touched: I didn't change any other finding from the audit. Nothing is
    committed.

> Now fix async error handing so that invalid requests or db errors cannot
  crash the api. Add central error middleware and consistent http responses.
  Add tests also.

● The plan is to wrap every route handler so rejected promises reach one error
  middleware that maps errors to safe, consistent JSON. I'll validate ids and
  bodies up front so bad input gets a 400 instead of reaching Prisma. And the
  tests will exercise the real Express app over HTTP, with no new dependency.
  First, the shared pieces.

      21 +  let n: number;                                               
      22 +  if (typeof value === 'number') {                             
      23 +    n = value;                                                 
      24 +  } else if (typeof value === 'string' && /^\d+$/.test(value))

      27 +    return null;                                               
      28 +  }                                                            
      29 +

         +null;

      67  
      68  A held seat shows as unavailable to everyone else until the hol

      69  
      70 +Every error response is JSON shaped `{ "error": "message" }`: 4
         +00 for malformed input, 401 not logged in, 403 not allowed, 404
         + not found, 409 conflict, 503 when the database is unreachable 
         +and 500 for anything unexpected (details are logged on the serv
         +er, never sent).

      72  Send the token as `Authorization: Bearer <token>`.

● Invalid requests and database errors can no longer crash the API. Typecheck
  passes and all 86 server tests pass. That is 27 new ones plus the earlier
  suites, run twice with a database. On the Docker server, the three requests
  that used to kill the process now return clean 400s. A full database outage
  returned 503s, /health stayed up, and /events returned 200 again once the
  database was back.

  How it works
  - Wrapper: asyncHandler.ts (server/src/lib/asyncHandler.ts) passes a rejected
    promise from any handler to next(err). All 12 routes use it. The
    per-controller try/catch blocks are gone, so errors are not handled in
    several places any more.
  - Central handler: errorHandler.ts (server/src/middleware/errorHandler.ts) is
    registered last, with a JSON 404 handler for unknown routes. It maps errors
    to a status and a safe message, and never forwards the raw message:

  | Error                                                 | Response
                           |
  |-------------------------------------------------------|---------------------
  --------------------------|
  | AppError (new, errors.ts (server/src/lib/errors.ts))  | its own status and
  message                    |
  | Malformed JSON or oversized body                      | 400 or 413
                           |
  | Prisma duplicate, missing row, foreign-key conflict   | 409, 404, 409
                           |
  | Database unreachable or pool and transaction timeouts | 503
                           |
  | Prisma validation error                               | 400
                           |
  | Anything else                                         | 500 Internal server
  error, logged server-side |

  - Strict input checks: malformed ids and bodies get a 400 before they reach
    Prisma. Ids from the URL and seatId must be positive 32-bit integers, and
    login and register require strings. This is what stopped GET /events/abc and
    a login with no password from reaching the database.
  - Safety net: server.ts (server/src/server.ts) logs stray unhandled rejections
    instead of exiting. Request errors already go through the middleware.

  Consistent responses: every error is { "error": "message" }. The old message
  and msg keys, the plain-text 500 and the 200 {success:false} for a missing
  event are gone. The README documents the format.

  Behavior changes to know about
  - Missing event: a missing event now returns 404 instead of 200.
  - Bad ids: a malformed booking id on confirm now gives 400 instead of 404.
  - Duplicate email: registering one gives 409 Email already registered, instead
    of the raw database text.
  - Failed bookings query: /bookings/mine now returns a real error when its
    query fails. It used to return 200 with [], which looked like "no bookings".
  - Client: it already read data.error in most places, so the booking, hold and
    login messages simply improve. Two client problems remain:
    - EventDetailPage still can't handle a missing event, because it expects
      seats in the response.
    - CreateEventPage shows the raw JSON as its error text.

    I left the client alone. Say if you want both fixed.

  Tests
  - The new errorHandling.test.ts (server/test/errorHandling.test.ts) runs the
    real Express app over HTTP, with no new dependency. Database failures are
    simulated by making Prisma methods reject. It covers:
    - the one error shape
    - malformed input of every kind
    - each Prisma error mapping
    - no leaking of secrets in 500 responses
    - a 25-request burst of failures with the API still up
  - The requireAuth tests now expect a 401 AppError passed to next, and
    toPositiveInt has its own cases.
  - With the wrapper's .catch(next) removed, 37 tests fail and Node reports the
    same unhandled rejections that used to kill the process. With the 500
    response made to leak the raw message, 3 tests fail. Both are restored.

  I haven't committed anything.

● A user can now cancel only their own bookings. Typecheck passes, all 103
  server tests pass (17 new), and the live checks on the Docker server behave as
  expected. That includes my 34-check regression script.

  What changed (bookings.service.ts and bookings.controller.ts):
  - Ownership in the update: the cancel query now matches on booking id, owner
    and CONFIRMED status together. A booking that isn't yours is never modified,
    even if two requests race.
  - No match: the service looks up why nothing was cancelled, so each case gets
    its own status code:

  ┌──────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │          Situation           │                 Response                 │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Your own confirmed booking   │ 200 {success:true}                       │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Your own booking, already    │ 200 {success:true}, nothing changes, so  │
  │ cancelled                    │ a retried request is harmless            │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Someone else's booking or    │ 403 You can only cancel your own         │
  │ hold, organizers included    │ bookings                                 │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Booking id doesn't exist     │ 404 Booking not found                    │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Your own hold (not a         │ 409 Only confirmed bookings can be       │
  │ booking)                     │ cancelled                                │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ No token, or a bad or forged │ 401                                      │
  │  token                       │                                          │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Malformed id (abc, 1.5, -1)  │ 400                                      │
  └──────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Tests (cancelBooking.test.ts (server/test/cancelBooking.test.ts)): 17 tests
  run the real Express app over HTTP against Postgres. The unauthorized cases
  are:
  - another user gets 403 and the booking stays CONFIRMED with cancelledAt unset
  - an organizer is refused too
  - someone else's hold can't be cancelled
  - an attacker walking through three victims' booking ids gets three 403s and
    nothing is cancelled
  - the same check at the service level
  - an owner and an attacker cancelling at the same moment, where exactly one
    succeeds
  - no token, a garbage token, and a token forged with the old hardcoded secret
    all get 401

  The owner cases cover a normal cancel, a freed seat, a repeated cancel and
  cancelling a hold. Removing the ownership filter from the update makes 4 of
  these tests fail, so they would have caught the original bug.

  The existing hold-expiry test called cancelBooking with the old signature, so
  I updated that one call.

  Things to know
  - 403 vs 404: I used 403 for someone else's booking, since you asked for
    unauthorized cancellations. It confirms that the id exists, but ids are
    sequential and guessable anyway. 404 for both cases would hide that, and
    confirming a hold already works that way. It's a one-line switch if you
    prefer it.
  - My Bookings page: the page lists only the user's own bookings, so it still
    works, and a repeated cancel still returns 200. But it ignores the response
    and always shows "cancelled successfully", so it would also say that after
    an expired login or a server error. I didn't change the client. Say if you
    want it to check the response.

  Nothing is committed.

    longer reads role from the request body.

  How it behaves: if a request includes a role, the account is still created, as
  an attendee. The response shows role: "ATTENDEE", the stored row is ATTENDEE,
  and the token says ATTENDEE, so creating an event with it returns 403 Only
  organizers can create events. I chose to ignore the field rather than reject
  the request, so sign-up never breaks. Returning an error instead would be a
  one-line change if you'd rather signal it.

  - The schema, the token format and the role check on creating events are
    untouched.

    unaffected.

> Can a concurrent request allow a user to hold more than 2 seats at once rn?

● Short answer: no. The limit is enforced inside a database transaction, and it
  has been tested with parallel requests. Rather than rely on memory of earlier
  runs, I'll check the current code, run the limit tests, and fire a fresh burst
  at the live server.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● No. Right now one user cannot end up holding more than 2 seats, even with
  simultaneous requests. I confirmed that again just now:

  - Live burst: I started with Alice holding 0 seats, then fired 20 simultaneous
    hold requests for 20 different seats across 2 events. The responses were
    two 201s and eighteen 409s, and Alice ended with exactly 2 active holds. I
    deleted the test events afterwards.
  - Test suite: the 10 hold-limit tests pass. They include 10 parallel holds by
    one user, repeated bursts, and holds spread across events.

  Why it holds
  - Row lock: a hold request first locks that user's row (SELECT … FOR UPDATE).
    A user's concurrent holds therefore run strictly one at a time.
  - Count after the lock: inside that lock, the request counts the user's
    unexpired holds and inserts only if the count is below 2.
  - Fresh view of committed holds: the next request sees the previous hold
    already committed. That's how Postgres works at its default isolation level
    (READ COMMITTED), which the app doesn't change.
  - Only one place creates holds: nothing else in the code creates a held row.
    Direct booking creates confirmed bookings, and confirming a hold doesn't add
    one.
  - Several server processes: the lock lives in the database, so running more
    than one server instance doesn't weaken it.
  - Mutation check: earlier I removed the lock to confirm the concurrency tests
    fail without it. They failed on every run.

  What this does not prevent
  - Many accounts: registration is open with no rate limiting or email
    verification. Someone can make many accounts and hold 2 seats with each. The
    limit is per account, not per person.
  - Direct bookings: these have no cap at all, so one user can book every seat
    in an event. Only holds are limited.
  - Cycling seats: a user can hold 2 seats, let them lapse, and hold 2 different
    seats, and repeat indefinitely. That is allowed by design today.

  Under very heavy load, requests queued behind the lock could hit Prisma's
  5-second transaction timeout. They would get a 503 and would not hold an extra
  seat. These are the issues I noted in the earlier review (M4, M5). I changed
  no files this turn.

    query are already strict from the error-handling work. I'll verify those
    with tests rather than rewrite them.

  them return the cleaned values so the controllers don't repeat the work. Auth
  first:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Auth and event creation now reject bad input with a 400 before it reaches the
  database. Typecheck passes and all 227 server tests pass (110 are new). On the
  Docker server, my live checks returned the expected statuses, and the earlier
  34-check regression script passes.

  Changes

  The validators stay in each module's existing *.validation.ts file. They now
  return the cleaned values, and the controllers pass those on.

  Auth (auth.validation.ts (server/src/modules/auth/auth.validation.ts))
  - Register:
    - All fields must be strings.
    - The email must be a valid address of at most 254 characters.
    - The password must be 8 characters or more and at most 72 bytes. bcrypt
      silently cuts anything longer, and the limit counts bytes.
    - The name is trimmed and at most 100 characters.
  - Email handling: emails are trimmed and lowercased on register and login.
    Before this, A@x.com and a@x.com were separate accounts.
  - Login: it applies none of the sign-up password rules, so existing short
    passwords still work. It only caps absurdly long values.

  Event creation (events.validation.ts
  (server/src/modules/events/events.validation.ts))
  - Text fields: title and venue must be text of 1 to 200 characters, trimmed.
    The description is optional and at most 5,000 characters.
  - Start time: startsAt must be a valid ISO date in the future. Garbage dates
    and past dates are rejected.
  - Grid size: rows and cols must be whole numbers of at least 1. The 26 x 30
    maximum is kept, with its existing message. Negative, fractional and string
    values used to get through.
  - Price: priceCents is optional and must be a whole number from 0 to
    100,000,000. The null that the form sends for an unparseable price now gets
    a 400. Before, it silently became a free event.
  - Other fields: unknown fields are dropped, and the organizer always comes
    from the token.

  Booking and route parameters: these were already strict from the
  error-handling work: positive 32-bit integers for seatId, route ids and the
  eventId filter. Rather than rewrite them, I added tests across every route
  that takes an id: abc, 1.5, 0, 1e3, 0x10, +5, spaces, Arabic digits,
  out-of-range numbers and a repeated eventId. All of these give 400 and none
  reaches the database.

  Tests are in validation.test.ts (server/test/validation.test.ts).
  - Validator tests: all three validators are tested across valid input,
    normalization, each rejection and the boundaries.
  - HTTP tests: these use Prisma stubs and check three things: every case
    returns the right status and message, a rejected request never reaches the
    database, and event creation stores the trimmed values.
  - Database test: one test against Postgres confirms that registering,
    registering a duplicate in another case and logging in using a different
    case all behave as one account.
  - Mutation check: with the email normalization, the password minimum and the
    future-date check removed, 9 tests fail.
  - Safer stubs: my first mutation run leaked a test user and a past-dated "Jazz
    night" event into the dev database. I deleted both and changed the stubs to
    throw if they are ever called. A broken validator now only fails tests and
    writes nothing, which I re-checked. Your own test@gmail.com user and "Test
    event" are untouched.
  - Existing tests: a few used the 7-character password pw12345 and an invalid
    date, so I changed those to valid values.

  Things to know
  - Mixed-case emails: any existing account with capital letters in its email
    would no longer match at login. Your seeded accounts are all lowercase, and
    the dev database currently has no mixed-case emails.
  - Create-event form: a typo in the price field now shows "priceCents must be a
    whole number between 0 and 100000000" instead of silently creating a free
    event. The form's toISOString() call on an empty date still throws before
    the request, as before. I left the client alone.
  - Sign-up form: a password under 8 characters now shows a clear error from the
    server on the sign-up form.

  Nothing is committed.

  such as the the password min/max, max name characters with how many left,
  indication of the fields of event creation etc.

  LoginPage and CreateEventPage will use it. Counters use maxLength where

  live counter and no maxLength.

● The sign-up and create-event forms now have a label above each field and hint
  text below it. The text fields also show a live "N left" counter. The client
  typechecks and builds. I also drove both pages in a simulated browser DOM,
  typing into the fields and reading the screen: all 36 checks pass. I haven't
  looked at it in a real browser, so spacing and colors are unchecked by eye.

  Sign-up form (LoginPage.tsx (client/src/pages/LoginPage.tsx))
  - Name: hint "Up to 100 characters" and a counter ("93 left", amber near the
    limit, "N over" in red). Typing past 100 is blocked.
  - Email: hint "Used to sign in. Capital letters don't matter."
  - Password: hint "At least 8 characters, up to 72 bytes (accents and emoji
    count extra)", and a counter in bytes, because the server limit is in bytes.
    The hint turns red under 8 characters or over 72 bytes. Eighty-character
    passwords of plain letters show "8 over", and € characters count 3 each.
  - Login mode: it only gets labels. The sign-up rules and counters are hidden,
    so users with older, shorter passwords see no warnings.

  Create-event form (CreateEventPage.tsx (client/src/pages/CreateEventPage.tsx))
  - Title and venue: "Required, up to 200 characters" with a counter.
  - Description: "Optional, up to 5000 characters" with a counter.
  - Date and time: "Required. Must be in the future." The picker won't offer
    past times, and a past value turns the hint red with "This is in the past".
  - Rows and seats per row: "Whole number, 1 to 26" and "1 to 30". They turn red
    when out of range, and a line below shows "40 seats in total (5 rows × 8
    seats per row)".
  - Price: labelled "Ticket price ($)" with the hint "In dollars, e.g. 15.00.
    Use 0 for free." It turns red for an empty, negative or non-numeric value.
  - Submit button: unchanged. The hints and red flags don't block submitting, so
    the server stays the authority.

  Shared pieces: FormField.tsx (client/src/components/FormField.tsx) is the
  shared label, hint and counter. limits.ts (client/src/limits.ts) holds the
  numbers, which copy the server's rules. If a server limit changes, update that
  file too.

  Things to know
  - Visual changes: every field now has a visible label in addition to its
    placeholder, so the forms are a bit taller.
  - Container: I installed jsdom in the client container for the simulated-DOM
    check, then removed it. package.json and the lockfile are unchanged.

  Nothing is committed.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Every important action in the client now opens a confirmation dialog before
  anything is sent. The client typechecks and builds, and 52 simulated-browser
  checks pass. I drove all three pages in a simulated DOM with a mocked server.
  I haven't clicked through it in a real browser, so the visual design of the
  dialog is unchecked by eye.

  Brief: where I changed

  New: ConfirmDialog.tsx (client/src/components/ConfirmDialog.tsx) is one
  reusable dialog that every action below uses.
  - Cancelling: Escape, "Go back" and clicking the backdrop all cancel without
    sending anything.
  - Focus: it starts on the safe button so a stray Enter can't confirm, and Tab
    stays inside the dialog.
  - Page behind: it can't scroll while the dialog is open.
  - While the request runs: both buttons lock and the confirm button reads
    "Please wait…". A double-click can't submit twice.
  - Styles: destructive actions get a red confirm button, everything else the
    normal gradient one.

  Event page: EventDetailPage.tsx (client/src/pages/EventDetailPage.tsx)
  - Book Now: it asks "Book seat A1?" with the event, seat, ticket price,
    service fee and total.
  - Hold for 10 minutes: it spells out the consequences before holding: nobody
    else can book it, you can hold at most 2 seats, and if the hold runs out the
    seat is no longer available to you.
  - Confirm in "Your Holds": it asks "Confirm seat A3?" with the price summary.
    If the hold expires while the dialog is open, the dialog closes by itself.
  - Not logged in: it still sends you to the login page.

  My Bookings: MyBookingsPage.tsx (client/src/pages/MyBookingsPage.tsx)
  - Cancel: the browser's plain window.confirm is gone. A red-themed dialog
    names the event, seat, date and price and says it can't be undone. The
    buttons are "Keep booking" and "Yes, cancel booking".
  - Confirm hold: it has a dialog like the event page's.

  Create event: CreateEventPage.tsx (client/src/pages/CreateEventPage.tsx)
  - Publish dialog: Launch Event opens "Publish this event?" with a summary:
    title, venue, start, seat count and price. It warns the event goes live
    right away and the seat grid can't be changed after publishing.
  - Checks before asking: obvious mistakes (missing title or venue, no or past
    date, bad grid, bad price) show a message and don't open the dialog, so you
    never confirm something the server would reject.

  Also touched: limits.ts (client/src/limits.ts) gained the hold length (10
  minutes) and the hold limit (2), which the hold dialog reads.

  Beyond what you asked

  - Cancel button on cancelled rows: it no longer appears there, since asking to
    confirm a cancellation of an already-cancelled booking made no sense.
  - Cancel result: the page now checks whether the cancel worked. It used to say
    "cancelled successfully" even when the server refused.

  Not covered

  - Logout, login and sign-up: they aren't destructive, so I left them without a
    dialog.
  - Selecting a seat: this changes nothing on the server, so it has no dialog
    either.

  Nothing is committed.

● The "cancelled" badge on My Bookings is now red. I also fixed two other colors
  that gave the wrong signal. The client typechecks and builds, and 12
  simulated-browser checks pass. I haven't viewed it in a real browser.

  What I changed

  1. Cancelled badge (MyBookingsPage.tsx (client/src/pages/MyBookingsPage.tsx))
     - Every booking badge was green with a ✓, including CANCELLED. Cancelled
       now shows a red "✕ CANCELLED". Confirmed stays green with "✓ CONFIRMED".
  2. "Seats left" badge on the events list (EventsPage.tsx
     (client/src/pages/EventsPage.tsx))
     - It was always green, even for sold-out and finished events, on both the
       featured card and the grid cards. It is now:

  | Case                                                        | Badge
        |
  |-------------------------------------------------------------|---------------
  -------|
  | Event already started                                       | grey "Ended"
        |
  | No seats left                                               | red "Sold out"
        |
  | Nearly full (3 seats or fewer, or 15% or less of the seats) | amber "N seats
  left" |
  | Plenty left                                                 | green "N seats
  left" |
     "1 seats left" is now "1 seat left". For example, your seeded "Intro to
     TypeScript" event showed a green "0 seats left" and now shows a grey
     "Ended".
  3. Event page header (EventDetailPage.tsx
     (client/src/pages/EventDetailPage.tsx))
     - The ENDED badge used the same purple as LIVE NOW. It is now grey. LIVE
       NOW is unchanged.

  Reviewed and left alone
  - Toasts and error messages are consistently green for success and red for
    errors.
  - The hold timer is amber, turning red under a minute.
  - The "(Required)" label is red, and "(Optional)" is grey.
  - The character counters turn amber near the limit and red when over.
  - The Cancel button and the destructive dialog are red, and the Confirm
    buttons are green.
  - Held seats are amber, and expired-hold seats are grey and dashed.

  Not a color, but worth knowing: on My Bookings, "Total Bookings", "Upcoming"
  and "Total Spent" count cancelled bookings too. A cancelled booking still adds
  to the amount spent. I didn't change it since you asked about colors. Say so
  if you want those totals to count only confirmed bookings.

  I removed the temporary test tooling from the client container afterwards.
  Nothing is committed.

  The change is in MyBookingsPage.tsx (client/src/pages/MyBookingsPage.tsx). The
  summary row now has four boxes: Total Bookings, Upcoming, Cancelled (new,
  shown in red) and Total Spent.

  - Cancelled: it counts the user's cancelled bookings.
  - Upcoming: it counts confirmed bookings that haven't started yet. Cancelled
    ones are left out, so cancelling an upcoming booking drops it by one.
  - Total Spent: it sums confirmed bookings only, so a cancelled booking is no
    longer counted as money spent. It adds up in cents, which avoids errors like
    $0.10 + $0.20 coming out slightly off.
  - Total Bookings: it still counts every booking, including cancelled ones. Say
    so if you want it to count only active ones.

  The numbers are now calculated from the bookings list every time, instead of
  being stored separately. They always match the list, and they update right
  after a cancel because the page reloads the list.

  Example from my test data (5 confirmed, 2 cancelled, one of the confirmed
  already past):

  ┌────────────────┬───────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┐
  │                │ Before cancelling one │ After cancelling a $5.50 one │
  ├────────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
  │ Cancelled      │ 2                     │ 3                            │
  ├────────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
  │ Upcoming       │ 4                     │ 3                            │
  ├────────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
  │ Total Spent    │ $19.10                │ $13.60                       │
  ├────────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
  │ Total Bookings │ 7                     │ 7                            │
  └────────────────┴───────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────┘

  The checks also cover cancelling everything (Upcoming 0, Spent $0.00) and an
  empty list (all zeros).

  I removed the temporary test tooling from the client container. Nothing is
  committed.

  Regressions found and fixed

  Two client pages crashed to a blank screen when the API returned an error. I
  reproduced it with 503, 500 and 401 responses.
  - Events page: the new JSON error responses from the server were being treated
    as a list of events. A database error blanked the page instead of showing
    the "Oops!" banner. It now checks res.ok and shows the banner.
  - My Bookings page: my last change (the stats boxes) made an existing weakness
    worse. A token that has expired used to leave the page on a spinner. After
    my change it showed a blank page, which would have hit everyone logged in
    before the secret change. It now shows "Couldn't load your bookings" with a
    Log in link for an expired session, or a Try again button otherwise.

  Both fixes are in EventsPage.tsx (client/src/pages/EventsPage.tsx) and
  MyBookingsPage.tsx (client/src/pages/MyBookingsPage.tsx). They are the only
  uncommitted changes.

  Unrelated modifications found

  Nothing harmful. The changes against the initial commit that aren't feature
  work:
  - migration_lock.toml: Prisma added its two-line "do not edit" header and
    removed the trailing newline when I ran a migration drift check. It's
    cosmetic.
  - server/package-lock.json: it drops one "dev": true flag on fsevents, an npm
    side effect. It was already modified before I started this work.
  - Stray folder: server/.vitest/, which I created, is removed.

  Remaining issues

  Security and robustness
  - Body-parser advisory (high): the server's express dependency has a
    denial-of-service advisory, fixed by npm audit fix.
  - React Router advisories (moderate): the client has two, fixed only by a
    forced, possibly breaking upgrade.
  - Public default secrets: compose still has a public fallback JWT secret and
    database password, and publishes the database port to the host. The
    Dockerfile seeds known accounts and runs the dev server as root.
  - Open items from the earlier audit:
    - no rate limiting
    - X-Powered-By is exposed
    - CORS allows any origin
    - the login token is kept in localStorage
  - Missing event index: there is no index on Booking.userId or Booking.seatId.
  - GET /events load: it loads every seat and booking on each call.

  Business rules
  - Direct bookings: these have no cap, so one user can book a whole event. Only
    holds are limited, and cycling through seats works around that.
  - Past events: a hold can be confirmed after its event has started.
  - Seat-lapse rule: the block is permanent, and the expired rows it depends on
    can't be purged.
  - 403 on cancel: a 403 for someone else's booking confirms that the id exists.
  - My Bookings stats: "Total Bookings" still counts cancelled bookings. That
    was a choice, not a bug.
  - Event list: the featured event is simply the first in the list, which can be
    a past event.

  Tooling
  - No lint tool: I'd add ESLint with a TypeScript preset.
  - Silent skips: 49 tests skip silently without a database, so a pipeline with
    no Postgres would look green. CI should run them against a real database.
  - No client tests: the client has no test setup. My UI checks ran in a
    throwaway DOM and are not saved in the repo.
  - Docker image: it only runs the dev server. There's no production stage,
    although the compiled server does run.

  I haven't committed anything. Let me know if you'd like the dependency
  upgrades, a lint setup or the CI step next.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● The high-severity body-parser issue is fixed. The dev and production secrets
  are now separated: the dev setup is unchanged for you, and the production
  setup has no defaults. Typecheck passes and all 246 server tests pass, twice.
  The live regression script passes 34 of 34, and the client builds. Your dev
  stack was rebuilt and is running with its data intact.

  1. Express / body-parser

  body-parser was pulled in by Express 4.22.3 at 1.20.8, with a
  denial-of-service advisory. I ran npm audit fix, which changed exactly three
  lines of package-lock.json (1.20.8 to 1.20.9). package.json is untouched, and
  Express itself stays at 4.22.3. npm audit now reports 0 vulnerabilities, both
  on the host and inside the rebuilt Docker image.

  2. Unsafe production secrets

  - Server refuses weak production secrets (env.ts (server/src/config/env.ts)):
    with NODE_ENV=production it won't start if JWT_SECRET is under 32 characters
    or a known placeholder. That covers the old hardcoded secret, the dev
    default and change-me, including when padded to 32 or more characters. It
    also rejects a database password that is empty or a known default. Local
    development is unaffected.
  - New production compose file (docker-compose.prod.yml): it has no defaults,
    and Compose refuses to render unless both POSTGRES_PASSWORD and JWT_SECRET
    are set. The database port isn't published, nothing is seeded, no source
    folders are mounted, and the API has a healthcheck.
  - Seed refused in production (seed.ts (server/prisma/seed.ts)): it won't run
    with NODE_ENV=production, because it creates accounts with a public
    password.
  - Dev compose file (docker-compose.yml): this stays as the local setup, now
    labelled as such. The only functional change is that the dev database port
    is bound to your machine only (IPv4 and IPv6 loopback).
  - Docs: the README has a new Production section.

  I split the compose files this way because Compose checks "required variable"
  markers in a base file even when an override replaces them. A ${VAR:?} in the
  shared file would have broken docker compose up locally.

  Verification

  - Dev stack: I rebuilt it from scratch. Login with the seeded accounts,
    authenticated calls, events and the client on port 5173 all work. The dev
    database is reachable from this machine and not from the LAN.
  - Production stack: I started it in an isolated project with its own volume
    and port, then removed it.
    - A dev-fallback or placeholder JWT_SECRET makes the server refuse to boot
      with the right message. The dev database password does the same.
    - With strong secrets it boots, healthy, in production mode, with no seeded
      accounts. The seed organizer login is refused.
    - The database port isn't published, and sign-up plus an authenticated call
      work.
    - Tokens signed with the old or dev secrets get 401, and seeding is refused
      inside the container.
  - New tests: 19 new tests in env.test.ts (server/test/env.test.ts) cover the
    production checks and confirm dev values still pass outside production.

  Things to know

  - Connection delay I caused and fixed: binding the dev database only to
    127.0.0.1 made localhost (which tries IPv6 first) slow, and 5 concurrency
    tests timed out. I added the IPv6 loopback binding and they pass again.
  - Production image is still not hardened: it runs the API through tsx as root,
    with dev dependencies installed. It works but isn't a minimal production
    image.
  - Production client: the production stack has no web client, so build it
    separately and serve it statically.
  - Dev compose caveat: the dev compose file keeps a public JWT fallback, so
    running it in production without NODE_ENV=production would not be caught.
    Use docker-compose.prod.yml for production.
  - Client advisories: the two moderate react-router advisories in the client
    remain, since they need a forced upgrade.
  - Uncommitted: the pending EventsPage and MyBookingsPage error-handling fixes
    from the previous step are still uncommitted, and nothing here is committed.

● Booking.userId needed an index, so I added one. Booking.seatId did not, so I
  left it alone. Typecheck passes, all 249 server tests pass (246 plus 3 new,
  run twice), and the live API script passes 34 of 34. Your dev data is
  untouched.

  I measured this on a scratch database with about 130k bookings, 20k users and
  120k seats. I used EXPLAIN ANALYZE on the ten query shapes the app runs, and
  dropped the scratch database afterwards.

  userId: needed (added)

  - Before: /bookings/mine scanned the whole table, at 8.0 ms and growing with
    it. The hold-limit and lapsed-hold checks run on every hold and booking.
    They only worked because the (status, expiresAt) index let them read every
    expired row, about 1 ms. That grows without limit, since expired rows are
    never deleted.
  - After (userId, status): /mine takes 0.13 ms. The checks and the holds and
    blocked-seats lists take 0.03 to 0.05 ms, no matter how many expired rows
    pile up.

  seatId: not needed (not added)

  - Runtime queries: the booking lookups by seat already use the partial unique
    index on active bookings, each in 0.03 to 0.25 ms.
  - Foreign-key checks: a plain seatId index would only speed up deleting seats
    or events. That drops from 1,140 ms to 5 ms for a 300-seat event, but
    nothing in the app does it. The test setup only deletes tiny events.
  - Cost: write cost was not measurable, since inserts took 700 to 850 ms for
    20k rows with or without it. The index would be about 3 MB per 130k rows.
  - If you add event deletion later: adding it is one line, @@index([seatId]),
    plus a migration.

  What changed

  - Schema: schema.prisma (server/prisma/schema.prisma) gets @@index([userId,
    status]) on Booking, with a comment saying why seats have none.
  - New migration: 20261029165640_booking_user_status_index is a single CREATE
    INDEX. I added a new migration rather than editing the existing ones,
    because those are already applied, and editing them would change their
    checksums and make prisma migrate dev ask for a database reset.
  - Verification: Prisma generated it without trying to drop the partial index
    it can't model. A drift check came back empty, and a database built from
    scratch with all four migrations has the right indexes.
  - New test: indexes.test.ts (server/test/indexes.test.ts) has 3 tests that the
    per-user index, the sweeper index and the partial unique index exist. It
    fails when I drop the new index.

  Things to know

  - Building the index: it briefly blocks writes to Booking. That is instant at
    this size. On a very large table you would build it by hand with CREATE
    INDEX CONCURRENTLY, because Prisma migrations can't do that.
  - Event list: GET /events is still the real scaling problem. It loads every
    seat and booking, so no index helps it. That is a separate fix.

  Nothing is committed.
